### Implemented country availability (HEAD 0daa512)

- New `app/utils/marketAvailability.js`: authenticated Storefront product-level `availableForSale` in explicit `@inContext(country:...)`; verifies actual country, product/node identity, pagination, duplicates, availability booleans and HTTP/GraphQL errors. Complete country catalog scan initially; later changed-product `nodes(ids:...)` deltas add restocked products and remove unavailable/unpublished/deleted ones. Parameterized GraphQL variables, no shopper-supplied eligibility lists. Empty country eligibility is a real empty collection, not fallback to global stock. Existing variant selection/pinned gallery-first behavior and native market price verification are separate and preserved; **this is product-level stock, not a new per-country variant-matching engine**.
- New server service `app/services/marketAvailability.server.js`: `marketAvailabilityQueueV1`, concurrency **2 in the existing dedicated background process**, separate from the ranking queue. Initial scans and Shopify stock reads run only in background. Metadata proofs cached in Redis; a prepared page reads small metadata, **no per-card Shopify stock requests or full country-ID-list reads**. Full ID lists load only for a canonical cache miss/preparation. Extra production latency is not yet measured.
- Two ADDITIVE schema tables: `MarketStorefrontConnection` (server-only token, countries, locale) and `MarketAvailabilitySnapshot` (shop+country eligible IDs, connection identity, stock epoch, proof). Migration `prisma/migrations/20261026000000_market_availability/migration.sql`. Tokens are separate from public/global settings; loader selects only countries/date. Existing first50 cards still live in **the same physical CollectionLoadingSnapshot table**, with country-specific keys.
- Authenticated setup page `app/routes/app.settings.market-availability.jsx`, linked from General settings. `shopify.app.toml` adds OPTIONAL `unauthenticated_read_product_listings`. User authorized **adding Shopify permission and automatic token setup**, no manual API key. App Bridge response is `result==='granted-all'` with `detail.granted`. Server verifies granted scopes, reads tokens under **`shop { storefrontAccessTokens(...) }`**, reuses its own titled token or creates it, validates catalog visibility, then stores connection and queues countries. A connection that cannot see known active products fails clearly instead of hiding everything. Unconnected stores retain legacy behavior.
- **Activation still requires** backend/worker schema rollout, Shopify app configuration publication, then merchant approval via **Settings → General → Country availability → Connect**. A successful EC2 deployment alone does not publish Shopify optional scopes or prove the token flow works live. Shopify CLI **build** was performed; no agent CLI **deploy**. New token activation has not been tested live.
- Background discovers enabled countries every **12 minutes**, adds new country jobs and removes obsolete repeat schedulers; unchanged countries keep their proofs when the country list changes. Periodic full stock scans also every12min catch changes without product webhooks. Proof older than15min returns pending until refreshed. Per-request Shopify timeout30sec, scan budget10min, per-country lock30min. Failed queue jobs have cooldown; retries can use saved unserved scan bases plus deltas.
- Product/inventory webhook worker explicitly invalidates country availability after successful sync **even if global quantity/index fingerprint did not change**. Atomic Redis INCR+ZADD records stock epoch and changed IDs. Up to1000 changed product IDs use deltas; resets/missing history/larger batches use full scans. Epoch/connection checks reject racing scans as current proofs. A stock event may make requests pending until consistent refresh finishes; never claim instantaneous Shopify-to-storefront propagation.
- Country proof hashes change only when eligible IDs/context change. `marketStockWarmVersion` marker schedules that country's catalog collections after a new/recovered proof, and lets a failed warmup scheduling step retry without repeating Shopify stock reads. Unchanged eligibility avoids unnecessary collection rebuilds. **Changed eligibility rebuilds the country's collection catalog**, not precise differential product-to-collection publication. Do not claim targeted merchandising invalidation.
- `api.filter.jsx` applies country IDs before ranking/count/facet/pagination and intersects eligible pins so they cannot bypass country stock. Country eligibility takes precedence over the legacy global-stock flag. Independent config bootstrap bypasses country preparation. Working/published card copies carry matching country proofs; stale copies fall back, stale/cross-country page tokens409/restart page1, unprepared stock202 rather than empty200/global fallback. Snapshot/session adapters are in `collectionCardStore.js`, `collectionPublishedPage.js`, `collectionCards.server.js`, `collectionPreparation.server.js`. Request fingerprints include stock proof; country remains in saved-page URL key.
- Follow-up reviews still appropriate: permission revocation/uninstall repeat-scheduler cleanup was not exhaustively audited, and genuinely live setup/country coverage/latency still require acceptance checks. Do not claim all features/all edge cases or all market snapshots were live-verified.

### Two data layers and update behavior

- The proposed two layers are implemented logically in ONE physical PostgreSQL `CollectionLoadingSnapshot` table using `kind=ranking`, `cards`, `published`. Background preparation builds full canonical ordered IDs and precomputes **50 complete cards plus full-collection facets/settings** (`rankingCardsPrecompute:true`, limit50). `collectionPublishedPage.js` publishes a complete snapshot; visitor receives 25 initially and next25 only on Load more. Page3 starts at position51 using the same full ranking session.
- Published snapshots retain the last complete publication while a replacement builds. `collectionPublishedSession:${shop}:${token}` keeps the first50 snapshot consistent for page2; `collectionRankingSession:${shop}:${token}` freezes full ordered IDs; both last3600sec. A background publication does not automatically replace an existing published shopper session; a subsequent visit/reload gets the new publication when ready. Independent pinned-prefix previews can hand off to canonical page1; identical cards retain DOM and verified prices.
- A new first-position pin or merchandising edit is **not guaranteed instantly visible**: old publication may serve until replacement is complete. Current rule saves broadly invalidate shop state and rebuild. Identifying exact rule diffs and updating only affected collections/products was discussed, **not implemented**. Do not claim targeted incremental publication. Removals, boosts, bury and Include/Exclude can alter many IDs, counts and facets; publish consistent replacements.
- Prices are verified separately with Shopify product `.js` responses on render, cached per page/market for60sec; no continuous price polling added. Saved price-range facets update with snapshot refresh. ### Current implementation and correctness contract

- Canonical native best-selling comparator puts explicit configured pins ahead of tag scoring, boosts, Bury and inclusion tiers. Canonical merchandising Exclude conditions protect explicit pins; do not introduce a stricter replacement contract. Route eligibility retains its active/in-stock/imported collection membership/Include scope semantics. Custom sorts can outrank pins and are excluded from this proof path. Canonical final zero-base-price partition can move pins behind paid products: selected leading pins must have known finite positive **synced base prices**. Market price verification remains separate and is never replaced with estimated/saved prices on screen. Later pins beyond position 25 with zero prices do not invalidate an otherwise proven paid first page; canonical pagination still handles their actual positions.
- `app/utils/collectionPinnedPrefix.js`: choose first **up to 25 eligible configured pins in explicit order**, rejecting malformed/duplicate pin configuration and unproven current shop/status/title/handle/price documents. Read only those selected documents, run the actual canonical ranking/merchandising/comparator on the bounded set for matching order and image metadata, and decline safely on a mismatch. No heuristic native-ID remainder under arbitrary tag scoring. General zero/few-pin cold first-page ranking remains unresolved. Missing native-order snapshot or a failed/unavailable full-ranking queue need not block an independently proven head. `deferDirect` skips the optional whole-ranking direct attempt before eligible preview heads; canonical follow-up retains normal retry/error behavior.
- `app/routes/api.filter.jsx`, `collectionCards.js`, `collectionPreparation.server.js`, `collectionPreparation.js`: only public unfiltered native best-selling initial previews opt into the head. Saved verified canonical cards can return immediately; head is used when full ranking is pending/failed. V3 response is HTTP 200, `X-CFP-Collection-Cards: prefix`, `cfpCards.version:1`, `preview:false`, `prefix:true`, **proof.version:2** with 64-hex inputVersion, null rankingToken and null totalCount. It is never saved as a full ranking/card snapshot. Normal ready pages and head pages carry complete product/variant/card data, streamed with existing NDJSON/sync-flush gzip when negotiated; JSON fallback retained. Reader caps records at **25** and validates proof/count/order/truncation. Old clients and q=0 do not receive unsupported heads.
- Head validity covers shop/market/sort/pins/rules/configuration, native-order revision (including initially missing order), schedules, global/collection content and hard-data revisions, runtime generation. Checks run around preparation and before delivery. `app/utils/collectionHeadProofs.js` keeps bounded Redis proof IDs/version for ten minutes (1024 per shop; deterministic same-millisecond retention via monotonic Lua score). Canonical worker/route shadow comparison logs match, changed-input skip or mismatch with position; mismatches block that shop/input version for one hour. Optional telemetry failures must not break canonical ranking. Comparison is best effort, not a guarantee every closed-browser/racing head gets compared.
- `extensions/theme-extension/assets/collectionFilterPro.js`: prefix stays visible during canonical preparation. Canonical page one adopts the proper token, sets page=1, updates known count and enables Load more. When product JSON is unchanged, update count/pagination **without replacing DOM or verified prices**. Page two is fetched only after shopper activation. `renderPagination` also refuses incomplete known first pages. Full cards verify market prices independently immediately; head cards are no longer permanently lightweight image/title placeholders. Inline generated script wrappers from earlier parser fix remain correct; do not reintroduce raw JS or double script tags.
- `app/utils/collectionCardStore.js` uses `acceptsCollectionPinnedPrefix(request)` to force full 25-document validation for V3; `collectionCardValidation.js` five-document preview applies only to legacy callers. Regression proves a change in card 11 rejects the complete cached page even while the legacy first-five check can still pass.
- Background-only candidate index reads: `browseConcurrency` defaults to 1, bounded at 4; `collectionRuleFacts.server.js` uses 4 only when populating background facts, direct visitor reads stay at 1. ## Background-cache failure and rollback — 2026-10-13

- User deployed caching commit db4fec8 and extension version 314. Chrome on Dress Outlet Formal Dresses, unpublished theme 193281622387, showed repeated HTTP 202, Shopify snapshot ready but collectionRankingStatus pending, followed by Retry and zero cards. Deployment run 38354828772 succeeded; deployment success did not establish queue/runtime safety.
- User explicitly requested reverting first, then continuing diagnosis. Restored every application/extension/script/test/README change in db4fec8 to parent 4bfbfe6, deleting its nine new files and preserving historical AGENTS.md entries. App/extensions/scripts/tests/README now match 4bfbfe6 exactly. This is LOCAL, UNCOMMITTED and UNDEPLOYED; backend AND worker plus Shopify extension rollback still require publication. Never claim the live cards are recovered from this local rollback.
- Read-only diagnostics: ec2 run 38355291560 showed no app/worker restarts or OOM. Queue runs 38355407311, 38355620228 and 38355959040 showed failures reporting Unknown collection ranking job. Raw Redis failed job IDs collection-warm-thedressoutlet.myshopify.com-1/-3/-7/etc remained in the failed sorted set while their hash name/data/failure fields were absent. The invalidator scans *shop-domain* and excludes only snapshots/sessions/revisions/warmup-context keys; it does NOT exclude bull: queue job hashes. Since warmup job IDs contain the shop domain, later invalidations delete their metadata. This directly identifies a code-level queue-data deletion flaw consistent with the observed unknown jobs; do not blame missing images or OpenAI.
- Revisions continued advancing during builds: 73 at 06:12:28 UTC, 96 at 06:14:55, 129 at 06:18:37. At the last raw check the active preparation spec was revision 126 while current revision was 129, so its guard would discard publication. Continuous shop-wide product invalidation can prevent a complete ~15-second build from ever being served. The exact producer of each invalidation was not traced.
- Local fixture headless tests and isolated real-engine tests did not exercise actual BullMQ job metadata keys alongside invalidation, or ongoing production invalidations. Their passing results do not establish safety under those conditions. Future design must limit deletion to owned cache namespaces, preserve ALL queue infrastructure, and avoid rebuilding unrelated collection rankings on every shop product change; validate with real isolated BullMQ/Redis and concurrent updates before restoring background preparation.
- Temporary diagnostic branch codex/ranking-worker-diagnostic-20261013 was removed after reading results. No production queue/index/server/admin settings were changed during these diagnostic workflows. Keep the rollback in place; do not reintroduce the caching implementation based on passing mocked tests.

## Background ranking and invalidation fixes — 2026-10-13

- User authorized applying the loading/cache fixes after reviewing ranking freshness. Local implementation moves complete non-manual ranking preparation into a dedicated BullMQ collectionRankingQueue, concurrency one, separate from webhook/sync processing. Cache misses return bounded-polling pending responses instead of keeping the storefront request open for the scan. Prepared results use compact canonical SHA-256 keys, ten-minute TTL and proactive refresh on access within the final two minutes. Visitor jobs take priority over warmups; repeated misses share the durable job. No bulk ID filters, parallel collection scans, early-fetch changes, facet cache, or price-cache peek from the rolled-back performance commit were restored.
- Monotonic Redis shop revisions advance on invalidation. Preparation checks revisions before/after building and after publication; obsolete results cannot serve new sessions. Config cache keys also include distributed revisions, preventing a late write from another process from resurrecting old settings. Existing immutable browsing tokens remain valid for their original sequence. Cache deletion uses SCAN and preserves snapshots, tokens, revisions and warmup contexts.
- Pin editor saves and collection pin delete/bulk-delete/duplicate/toggle paths now invalidate; batch pin imports invalidate after successful writes. Pin status updates are shop-scoped. Product/collection deletes now invalidate too. Shopify order refresh queues warmup only when its content revision changes. Merchandising save invalidation already existed. Shopify manual drag-and-drop sort remains unchanged/excluded.
- Up to 20 anonymous, unfiltered page-one contexts per shop are remembered for one day and warmed after invalidation/changed Shopify order, using fresh route configuration, never replaying customer tags, filtered/token requests or duplicate query parameters. A truly cold/expired ranking still takes time to build; a long worker backlog or continuing invalidations can exhaust polling and show Retry. No stale explicit-rule arrangement is substituted for new visitors. Shopify snapshot/webhook delays still apply.
- Frontend polls ranking readiness as well as Shopify readiness, retains cancellation/retry limits, and adds a loading status after four seconds. Cards, images and market prices remain independently rendered. Pending backend responses retain the older extension's readiness signal to avoid showing an empty grid before extension rollout. Existing staggered reveal and filter stacking fix were already committed in 4bfbfe6; the new work does not redeploy them.
- Verification: 249 search/webhook/sync tests passed, including new cache/job lifecycle, cross-process revision race, admin pin invalidation, warmup privacy, readiness and worker isolation cases. JS syntax, CSS parsing and diff checks passed. Build logs: ignored scratch/ranking-loading-build.log and ranking-loading-tests.log.

## Semantic search: OpenAI active, Gemini retained - 2026-10-12

- User explicitly requested retaining both provider setups in code, with ONLY OpenAI active now. Gemini is reserved for a future explicit switch. No cross-provider automatic fallback: OpenAI failure should fall back to keyword search, never Gemini.
- Existing hybrid search code was committed earlier (`5b1ccb1`, `339ecef`): 50/50 semantic/keyword blend in full search and multiSearch, preserving tenant filters, facets, pagination and explicit sorting. Former setup and relay were Gemini-only under embedder name `default`; provisioning was deliberately gated to avoid restarting a catalog-wide migration during sync.
- New local code defaults `SEMANTIC_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER` to `openai`; supports explicit `gemini`; rejects other names. Uses OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small`, 768 dimensions, native Meilisearch `openAi` source unless a private relay is configured. Official API reference verified: `https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/embeddings` and `/api/reference/resources/embeddings/methods/create`.
- `app/utils/semanticSearch.js` now supplies OpenAI, Gemini and provider-aware relay configurations. Active embedder names are `openai` or `gemini`, not legacy `default`, so vectors cannot be reused between providers. Provisioning removes legacy `default` and the inactive provider; Gemini indexing is disabled when OpenAI is selected.
- `app/services/semanticSearch.server.js` checks readiness for the selected provider only, with provider-specific cache keys. A legacy/default Gemini embedder cannot activate OpenAI queries. Semantic queries retain 0.5 ratio and keyword fallback.
- `app/services/embeddingRelay.server.js` supports OpenAI `/v1/embeddings` batches and existing Gemini batches, validates 768-dimensional finite vectors, restores OpenAI response index order, retains authentication/body limits/concurrency/retries, and isolates caches/pending requests by provider and hashed credential. Relay configuration embeds a provider marker. Legacy unmarked requests belong to Gemini and return 409 when OpenAI is selected, preventing vectors from entering the wrong provider's index. Provider errors are sanitized.
- Sync provisioning now uses the selected provider, still gated by `SEMANTIC_EMBEDDING_SETUP_ENABLED=true`. Keep this flag unset after initial setup; ordinary sync should not initiate full migration.
- Added `scripts/setup-semantic-search.mjs`: `npm run semantic:setup` provisions the selected provider and waits for the catalog embedding task. `npm run semantic:status` reads configuration without printing keys. Script loads local `.env` if present; run on a server with access to the private Meilisearch host. Setup can re-embed the entire shared product catalog. README documents provider selection, setup, relay and future switching.
- Required now: `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Optional explicit `SEMANTIC_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=openai`. Optional relay needs BOTH `SEMANTIC_RELAY_URL` and `SEMANTIC_RELAY_TOKEN`. Future Gemini requires `SEMANTIC_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=gemini`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, deployment/restart and rerunning setup to rebuild its vector space. `SEMANTIC_SEARCH_ENABLED=false` disables hybrid queries.

## Search color and variant images ? 2026-10-12

- User requested a universal search color/image fix after `blue wedding dress for brides mother` returned unrelated colors on Dress Outlet. Read-only Dress search verification is authorized; do not modify its admin or SoBooster settings.
- New local fix scopes query-parser facets and aliases to the requested shop. Meilisearch uses one shared `products` index with shop predicates, not a separate index per shop. The earlier global parser dictionary was an isolation defect; it now uses shop-scoped Redis cache keys, active-product facets, and configured Color/Colour aliases.
- Explicit color intent is a hard filter in instant search and all full-search tiers/facets; zero matching catalog colors does not broaden to other colors. Wrong-color pins are excluded before pin pagination validation. Compound colors such as Slate Blue match Blue; Navy/Periwinkle require configured aliases to Blue.
- Search cards use a matching available variant when possible, its link and market price, and its color image. Same-color sister variants may supply a missing image; unrelated product images and unverified hover images are suppressed for explicit color results. Color/Colour and option positions 1?3 are supported.
- Backend and both Shopify extension assets need deployment. These changes are local; no live fixed-behavior claim or deployment has been made. Tests and build should be checked against the final worktree before reporting.


## Search color alias follow-up ? 2026-10-12

- User screenshot 163930 query `blue wedding dress for mother of bride` still showed Deep Green, Champagne and Gold. Fresh production API confirmed the new selectedVariantId/searchColor metadata was deployed, but explicitly selected those unrelated colors. ## SoBooster comparison and authorized loading strategy - 2026-10-12

- User restored Chrome access and authorized testing only Another Shopify Store. Compared SoBooster preview p50wv31edjpk6kzm (Senior Assessment theme 131486187602) and our preview zwqsdsbrxk8q5e32 (theme 123522449490). Dress Outlet tabs were not inspected in this browser comparison.
- Automated Collection: SoBooster API 152 ms, our API 420 ms; valid product cards inserted at approximately 1.63 s and 1.74 s. Price ascending updated card order in 353 ms vs 979 ms; vendor filter Another shpyfy Store updated in 326 ms vs 503 ms. All 19 initial and price-sort products and the 13 vendor-filtered products matched in sequence. These are individual measurements, not production benchmarks; themes differ and collections are small.
- Accessories: SoBooster API 222 ms, our API 326 ms, cards inserted at 2.19 s vs 2.32 s. SoBooster places Hydrogen before Liquid in the final zero-price group; ours places Liquid before Hydrogen. The same difference was observed in Hydrogen. This sequence difference was reported, not changed by performance work.
- Browser evidence: SoBooster starts its collection search alongside usf.js loading; one paginated response supplies products, variants with prices, facets and totals, advertising public max-age=15. It did not fetch per-product Shopify .js prices for collection cards; the four such requests in its theme were for other recommendation products. Our page fetched prices for every collection card after rendering. SoBooster's private ranking/index preparation remains unknown.
- User explicitly authorized adopting this strategy only if existing features remain intact. New local implementation starts an exact unfiltered first-page collection request in the inline script, validates its complete context before reusing it, forwards cancellation, and retains pending-order polling. Filtered URLs and later pages use the full state parser. Fresh page-one requests omit old ranking tokens.
- Ranked page documents are now read in one shop-scoped Meilisearch ID query (batches of at most 200), reconstructed in immutable token order, retaining missing pin slots for DB hydration and rejecting inactive products. Complete collection ranking, exclusions and pin priority are unchanged; the earlier bounded parallel ranking scans remain part of this local work.
- Collection facet multi-search results cache for 15 seconds with shop and full query context; normal shop invalidation removes them. Ranked response bodies remain no-store so new visits still resolve current ranking. Verified storefront market prices can be read synchronously from a 60-second per-product cache scoped to shop, country, currency, locale, route and customer tags; repeat renders reuse them without displaying estimated indexed prices. New-market first visits still fetch verified Shopify prices; don't claim all per-product price requests were eliminated.
- 236 search/sync/webhook tests passed, including early-request context matching, bulk document order/isolation/inactive checks and verified-price cache isolation. Both changed theme JS files pass syntax checks; diff checks and production build passed. Backend plus collectionFilterPro.js, marketPricing.js and collectionFilterPro.liquid need deployment. ## Confirmed cause of failed performance push - 2026-10-12

- Supersedes the preceding unknown-cause note. User requested finding the exact failure. Main HEAD was rollback commit 98a0d14, whose Deploy to EC2 run 38249770188 succeeded; failed performance commit 51cfcd5 had also deployed successfully (run 38249178088). A successful deployment did not establish runtime query compatibility.
- Isolated read-only diagnostic run https://github.com/https-webvory-com/shopify-filter-app/actions/runs/38250659171 executed the actual 51cfcd5 rankedCollectionPage module against production Meilisearch, restricted to Another Shopify Store. Production getFilterableAttributes returned idFilterable=false and shopFilterable=true.
- Reproduced FAILED_PUSH_BULK_QUERY: invalid_search_filter, "Index products: Attribute id is not filterable." The optimized readRankedCollectionPage introduced shop + id IN filters; every nonempty ranked page using that path can fail regardless of pins or rules. Primary-key document lookup is supported without making id filterable; filtering by primary key is a separate index setting.
- With the same three Another-store product IDs, the rollback's getDocument path succeeded. The failed version's collection scan succeeded with 19 Automated Collection products. This identifies the bulk ID-filter path as the reproduced fault; it does not prove every other performance change was production-safe.
- sync.server.js declares id in intended filterableAttributes, but code deployment does not apply persisted Meilisearch settings automatically. The previous optimization assumed the live index matched source settings. Mock tests asserted the filter string and returned products without reproducing the index's filterable-attribute restrictions, so they missed this incompatibility.
- No production index settings, catalog data, Shopify/SoBooster settings or application code were changed during diagnosis. Diagnostic workflow was placed only on a temporary isolated branch, then the branch was removed; logs remain available. Rollback stays in place. If optimization resumes, check actual engine capabilities and retain the getDocument fallback before using id filters; do not update shared index settings without considering reindexing cost and explicit scope.
